[1;38;5;188mCLOUD CONSOLE HARDENING — WAVE 28: THE INSTRUMENT FIXED “I COULD NOT LOOK” AT
THE ROOT AND LEFT IT STANDING AT THE OBJECT — AND THE ENVIRONMENT IT RUNS IN IS
THE ONE PLACE IT CANNOT LOOK[0m
[38;5;232m────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────[0m

Wave 27 was the best-argued wave this epic has run. It refuted both of the
lead's headline findings, paid Standing Law 0 by hand with 57 re-parented rows,
and proved that the instrument certifying this epic's health was manufacturing
false defect reports out of a directory it could not read. Five of its six
person-facing slices are on main. One PR is open — #9356 — and it is the one
everything else reads through.

The lead offered this wave a binary: (a) give the CI job the history the
instrument needs, or (b) restructure the red control to assert clause (a) alone.
Both are defensible. Both are also incomplete, and this Paper's headline finding
is why: measured live, in this session, the WAVE-27-FIXED predicate — the one on
#9356, with both of its new refusals armed — still emits six verbatim “commit …
is not an ancestor of origin/main” sentences inside a shallow clone. Same
disease, same prose, one condition over, in the exact environment the instrument
runs in automatically.

[1;38;5;78mTHE FINDING: wave 27 named the shallow clone in its own fix's header comment,
and did not close it[0m

seal-predicate.mjs:453 is one line: `try { execFileSync('git', ['-C', REPO,
'merge-base', '--is-ancestor', commit, 'origin/main'], …); } catch {
problems.push(`commit ${commit} is not an ancestor of origin/main`); }`. The
catch is unconditional. Charter D318 enumerated exactly what it swallows — “not-
a-git-repo / no-origin-main / shallow-clone / unknown-sha” — and wave 27's fix
closed the first two and wrote the other two into a comment.

The wave-27 header comment says it out loud, verbatim: “the ancestry `catch` at
the top of `verifyCommit` swallows not-a-git-repo, no origin/main, a shallow
clone and an unknown sha into one identical claim about the PRODUCT.” The remedy
that follows checks that `.github/workflows/cloud.yml` exists and that `git rev-
parse --show-toplevel` resolves to REPO. A depth-1 CI clone satisfies both.

Measured live in this session, controlled pair, same live ledger, same minute,
nothing varied but the clone:

[38;5;214m▌[0m The instrument that certifies this epic reports “I could not look” as “I
[38;5;214m▌[0m found a defect” — the exact sin wave 27 fixed — in the one environment where
[38;5;214m▌[0m it runs without a human present. That is not a test failure. It is the sixth
[38;5;214m▌[0m standing-test clause, live, on the very instrument that authored it.

[1;38;5;78mTHE DIRECTION: one disease, two audiences — the console and its instrument both
stop asserting what they did not measure[0m

This wave has one sentence and it points in both directions at once. A system
that could not determine something must say so. It must not substitute a
plausible guess, a default, or the negative branch of a two-way test it was
unable to run.

Pointed at the INSTRUMENT, that sentence resolves the lead's binary by refusing
it. Option (a) alone leaves the predicate lying in every other shallow
environment — a container, a fork PR, an agent's `--depth 1`, any future job.
Option (b) alone leaves standing the fact the lead correctly identified as
bigger than the test: that every seal run under this workflow is structurally
unable to evaluate clause (b). The answer that pays both is the DISCRIMINATION:
teach the git legs to tell rc 1 (“not an ancestor”, an honest answer about the
product) from rc 128 / missing object / shallow repository (“I could not look”,
an honest answer about the environment). Once the predicate can tell those
apart, (b) becomes the correct shape for the control — it asserts the floor it
is actually about — and (a) becomes a verified environment declaration rather
than a workaround, because a regression back to depth-1 would then produce an
honest INFRA-FAULT the job can assert on.

Pointed at the PERSON, the same sentence is already sitting in the ledger as
open rows. A refused connection is reported as “A network step timed out. Retry
usually fixes this.” — the console did not classify the failure, so it guessed,
and the guess carries ADVICE. A person switches on “Deployment failed” alerts
across email and every chat channel, and the event has zero producers anywhere
in the repository — the console tells them they are subscribed to something that
cannot arrive. These are not clipping bugs. They are the epic's founding vision,
in its purest form, and they are the wave's person bodies.

[1;38;5;59mWhat the finished wave looks like[0m

[38;5;59m• [0m#9356 is MERGED, first and alone, and it merged because its control was
  restructured to assert clause (a) — the floor it exists to test — not because
  anything red was weakened. Nothing about the depth-1 environment changed to
  get it green.
[38;5;59m• [0mThe seal predicate has a THIRD refusal in the same discipline as its first
  two: HISTORY-UNAVAILABLE. Run it in a shallow clone and it exits 2 with a
  named code and zero invented findings, exactly as it now does in a git-archive
  extraction. Mutation-proved with a REAL `git clone --depth 1`, not a stub —
  the wave-27 lesson is that stubbing the leg only moves the failure.
[38;5;59m• [0mconsole-harness.yml stops being an environment the instrument cannot work in.
  The seal job declares the history it needs, and a self-check reds if that
  declaration silently reverts — a guard on the guard's environment, which is
  the class of defect this whole wave is about.
[38;5;59m• [0mThe D307 door guard is ARMED, not ruled on for a third wave. `surface` becomes
  an enum; the open rows are backfilled BY THE LEAD'S HAND at Decide (wave 27
  proved that pattern with 57 re-parents); the fence widening is a numbered
  charter decision written before a builder touches mutations.ex; and the
  refusal is scoped to this epic's parent_id so its blast radius is this epic,
  not the whole task system.
[38;5;59m• [0mAt least four — target five — slices where a PERSON is misinformed or cannot
  reach a control, led by the two rows where the console gives a confident wrong
  answer to a question it never asked.

[1;38;5;59mWhat this wave deliberately does NOT do[0m

[38;5;59m• [0mIt does not spend a slice on `.preview-url`. That premise failed smoke: #9357
  already covers it by inheritance and the committed CSS comment says so.
  Booking it would be paying twice for one fix.
[38;5;59m• [0mIt does not chase orphans to zero. 57 is stated with all three provenance
  facts and repaid only by rows that leave honestly. Wave 27 already made the
  largest Law-0 payment in the epic's history; a wave spent on ledger hygiene is
  the disease the charter names at waves 9/10/11.
[38;5;59m• [0mIt does not touch .github/required-checks.json, and it registers nothing.

[1;38;5;78mThe direction debate: three candidates, argued, one committed[0m

[1;38;5;59mRival A — “Finish the instrument, build the door” (the wish read literally)[0m

Take the lead's binary, pick one leg, and spend the rest of the wave on the D307
door guard as a full multi-slice build: the `surface` enum, the backfill, the
fence ruling, the refusal, the mutation proof. Clean, obedient, and it retires
the highest-leverage row in the epic.

WHY IT LOSES. Three counts. First, arithmetic: four instrument slices against a
floor of four person bodies means eight slices minimum, and this epic's own
history says a wave that spends 4-of-6 on instruments moves the row count the
wrong way (waves 9/10/11: +11, +16, +7). Second, the door guard's dependency
chain — enum → backfill → fence ruling → refusal — is SERIAL and cannot fan out
to parallel builders, so it converts a fan-out wave into a queue. Third and
worst, it accepts the false binary. Picking (a) or (b) at speed leaves the
predicate lying in every shallow environment that is not this one workflow, and
this Paper has now proved live that such an environment exists and that the
epic's own instrument is in it.

[1;38;5;59mRival B — “Pay Law 0 to zero and drive at the seal”[0m

orphans is 57 and a=FAIL is the only clause failing. Spend the wave closing,
forwarding and adjudicating residue — including the cch-w22-s7 split D325
already ruled — and get the epic within sight of an honest SEAL.

WHY IT LOSES. It is the epic's stated disease wearing a productivity costume.
The charter's vision is that the CONSOLE stops lying; a wave with zero person
bodies fails the ratio outright and would be the fourth restatement of a law
rather than a payment of it. It also mistakes the seal for the goal — D318
proved the predicate could SEAL an epic that does not exist, which means a
falling orphan count was never the thing worth optimising. HARVESTED ANYWAY:
this Paper quotes the reading with all three provenance facts, and the cch-w22-
s7 adjudication is booked as a Decide-phase ledger action for the lead, costing
zero builders.

[1;38;5;59mRival C — “One disease, two audiences” — COMMITTED[0m

Two instrument slices that pay the environment question properly plus the
discrimination that generalises it; one door-guard slice made buildable by
moving its backfill dependency onto the lead's hands at Decide; and four-to-five
person bodies chosen so that at least two of them are literally the same defect
the instrument has — a system asserting a fact it did not measure.

WHY IT WINS. It is the only candidate where the instrument work and the person
work are the SAME argument, which means the wave's finding transfers: the
sentence that fixes the seal predicate is the sentence a builder applies to
`connection refused`. It keeps the ratio at four-plus without inventing filler.
It refuses the lead's binary on evidence rather than on preference — the depth-1
run is measured, not reasoned. And it ships the door guard ARMED this wave by
removing the dependency that blocked it, rather than ruling on it for a third
consecutive wave.

[1;38;5;59mThe sharpest attack on the winner, and how the wave absorbs it[0m

[38;5;214m▌[0m “You are deferring the D307 door guard AGAIN. Wave 26 ruled on it, wave 27
[38;5;214m▌[0m filed it, and ‘build the enum, defer the refusal’ is exactly the shape that
[38;5;214m▌[0m lets wave 29 defer it a fourth time. Meanwhile you have talked yourself into
[38;5;214m▌[0m a THIRD instrument slice nobody asked for.”

TAKEN, and it changes the plan rather than being argued away. The wave does not
ship the enum and defer the refusal — it ships the refusal ARMED, and it removes
the blocker by re-classifying it. D324 gave four honest reasons the guard could
not ship: (i) no classifier, (ii) the fence widening is not a builder's call,
(iii) CI is post-hoc detection not refusal, (iv) shipping before the backfill
makes the guard's first act on main a refusal of legitimate filings. Reasons (i)
and (iv) are LEDGER work, and wave 27 proved conclusively that ledger work
belongs to the lead's hands at Decide — 57 re-parents, executed in minutes, with
the post-condition asserted on the destination roster. Reason (ii) is a numbered
charter decision, which is also a Decide-phase artifact. Reason (iii) argues FOR
the door, not against it. So the builder's slice is what remains: the enum
validation and the refusal, scoped to one parent_id, with a mutation proof that
shows it can both refuse and pass. If survey finds the backfill is larger or
messier than wave 27's re-parent — a real risk, since `surface` is populated on
24 of 343 rows and is free prose — the wave scopes the guard's population to
rows the backfill actually reached and says so, rather than arming a refusal
over rows it cannot classify.

On the third instrument slice: it is not a third, it is the CORRECT first.
Without the discrimination, both of the lead's options are workarounds, and this
Paper has the run output proving it. The wave's own ratio discipline still binds
— if the discrimination and the environment declaration cannot be shown to be
one coherent slice at Decide, the environment half merges with #9356 and the
wave spends the recovered budget on a fifth person body.

quoted[0m

the truncated WALK is. Ancestry degrades before the diff does.

repository[0m), on ref resolution ([38;5;217;48;5;232mrev-parse --verify --quiet origin/main[0m) and on

down.

[1;38;5;59m3. #9356's red is ONE assertion, and the workflow already forbade the shape that
produced it[0m

The failure is [38;5;217;48;5;232mseal-predicate.test.mjs:1273[0m — [38;5;217;48;5;232massert.equal(sealed.status, SEAL)[0m
— actual 1, expected 0, with the mutant's own token reading [1;38;5;145ma=PASS b=FAIL
c=PASS[0m. Clause (a), the only thing that control is about, PASSES in CI. And
[38;5;217;48;5;232mconsole-harness.yml:239-248[0m on origin/main already states, verbatim, that depth-
1 makes the predicate "manufacture a false NO SEAL out of a shallow clone" and
that "the test file is hermetic instead". Wave 27's empty-roster control is the
first and only exit-code-asserting run in the file that goes down the LIVE path.
It violates a contract the job had written down.

[1;38;5;145mThis flips both of the lead's options.[0m Option (b) is not a dodge — it RESTORES
the file's own stated invariant. Option (a) does not merely change clone cost:
[38;5;217;48;5;232mseal-predicate.test.mjs:35[0m sets [38;5;217;48;5;232mREPO = resolve(HERE, '../../../..')[0m and passes [38;5;217;48;5;232m-
-repo REPO[0m to ~15 spawns, so the suite is hermetic today [1;38;5;145monly by accident[0m —
because depth-1 makes every git read fail uniformly. Flip to fetch-depth: 0 and
clause (b) begins returning real answers about real epic commits, and the
suite's verdict silently becomes a function of history state with not one test
line changed. Option (a) alone converts a hermetic suite into a history-coupled
one.

[1;38;5;59m5. The D307 door guard is BUILDABLE but the direction's in-file precedent is

[1;38;5;145mThe direction said mutations.ex:470-545 already hosts a create-time door guard.
It does not.[0m That range is [38;5;217;48;5;232mensure_claim_not_dropped/4[0m, an UPDATE fence whose

whose own header comment names that hole. The [38;5;217;48;5;232mcreate[0m clause at [38;5;217;48;5;232m:132-158[0m has [1;38;5;145mzero[0m

would be [1;38;5;145mthe first guard ever wired into the create clause[0m — a charter-visible
widening, not an added line.

[1;38;5;145mWhat is easier than advertised:[0m [38;5;217;48;5;232msurface[0m IS visible at the guard point.
[38;5;217;48;5;232mincoming_content(attrs)[0m already exists and is already the argument passed to
both existing guards, and a committed test proves the flat Sanity envelope is

[1;38;5;145mWhat is harder:[0m [38;5;217;48;5;232msurface[0m is not a declared task field at all (zero hits in
[38;5;217;48;5;232mtasks/schema.ex[0m), and the identifier [38;5;217;48;5;232msurface[0m is ALREADY a reserved schema-DSL
keyword meaning field placement (body|sidebar) across every plugin schema JSON.
And the backfill does not exist as a mechanical artifact: a composite classifier
built from every structured field the ledger carries scored [1;38;5;145m6/15 (40%)[0m blind
against the ledger's own human-written labels — worse than the 67% false-refusal
rate D324 measured on the drafted predicate. 86 of 230 open rows (37%) are
undecidable from every field sampled.

[38;5;69m▌[0m PROVENANCE CORRECTION (D319). The Strategize line quoted "tree
[38;5;69m▌[0m dfdc31db96b8…". That is the COMMIT sha. The tree at that commit is
[38;5;69m▌[0m 3cf1bda5661b6a119d017d1c8582fd2a93f356ed. D319 asks for a tree sha precisely

[1;38;5;59m9. Honest negatives — three places the wave should NOT spend[0m

[38;5;59m• [0moverflow-guard.mjs separates environment from defect correctly: die() is used
  for CDP bring-up and measurement breakage, distinct from the failures[] path,
  and its navigating-catch is loop-bounded and throws on exhaustion.
[38;5;59m• [0mregistry.ex's ~20 `_ -> true|false` catch-alls are Repo.one() nil-vs-1
  matches, not unknown-to-definite collapses; barkpark_in_team? documents its
  fail-closed intent.
[38;5;59m• [0mapp.js's catch-to-null totalisers: the one consumer traced (presenceSlotHtml)
  renders "Roster unreachable" — honest about not knowing. The other two
  consumers are UNTRACED and are a verify question, not a filed slice.

Also negative on scope: [1;38;5;145mthe git/history axis is NOT a class.[0m seal-predicate.mjs
is the only instrument this epic owns that execs git at all. cssom-parity.mjs
weighed and REJECTED a git-derived baseline in committed prose, naming
origin/main-ref availability among the contexts it must survive; all three path-
escape scripts enumerate with [38;5;217;48;5;232mfind[0m citing D31. [1;38;5;145mSlice 2 stays single-file.[0m What
generalises is the DOCTRINE, and it has two working implementations already in-
tree: [38;5;217;48;5;232mcssom-parity.mjs:608[0m (throws on unreadable input) and [38;5;217;48;5;232mrequired-checks-
verify.sh[0m (hard-fails on a protection read it cannot make, deliberately, "an
unreadable input is a failure and never a skip").

[1;38;5;59mCorners no surveyor reached[0m

[38;5;59m• [0mWhat a REAL actions/checkout@v4 workspace contains. Every shallow measurement
  in this survey is from a local `git clone --depth 1` over file://.
  actions/checkout fetches a NARROW REFSPEC and a merge ref for PRs, so CI may
  present the MISSING-REF shape (rc 128 on origin/main) rather than the MISSING-
  OBJECT shape measured. A guard tested only against a depth-limited clone could
  still be green-by-construction on a real runner.
[38;5;59m• [0mWhether --is-shallow-repository reports true inside a git WORKTREE of a
  shallow repo — and inside the epic-cycle worktrees every builder in this epic
  works in. If it under-reports there, the guard is green by construction in the
  very place it will be built.

[38;5;59m• [0mTask DESCRIPTIONS for the 86 undecidable backfill rows. The 40% classifier
  accuracy is a floor on the fields SAMPLED, not a proof that no classifier
  works — this is the single experiment that could turn 'not mechanically
  producible' into 'producible'.

[1;38;5;78mWhat Decide cannot live without knowing[0m

[38;5;59m• [0mWhether the CI runner's shallow shape is MISSING-OBJECT or MISSING-REF. These
  need different probes, and every measurement so far is from a local clone that
  may present the wrong one. This decides the discrimination's shape, not just
  its wording.
[38;5;59m• [0mWhether --is-shallow-repository is true inside a git worktree of a shallow
  repo — because that is where the builder will test the guard.
[38;5;59m• [0mWhether the clause-(a) lies (truncation floor, bucket-(c) cardinality floor,
  curl --fail) fold into the HISTORY-UNAVAILABLE slice as one coherent "the
  predicate stops asserting what it did not measure" change, or whether they are
  a separate slice. This is the wave's ratio pivot: folding them recovers a
  person body.
[38;5;59m• [0mWhether flipping console-unit to fetch-depth: 0 changes any test verdict
  TODAY. Cheap to produce (run the suite in a full clone and a depth-1 clone,
  diff per-test results) and it IS the exact blast radius.
[38;5;59m• [0mWhether the backfill is mechanically producible from task DESCRIPTIONS. If
  yes, the door guard arms over a real population; if no, it arms over the rows
  the backfill reached and the wave says so.
[38;5;59m• [0mWhether :agent_unreachable's :detail is ever non-empty at its dispatch site —
  this decides whether the fifth person body is one event or two.
[38;5;59m• [0mWhich function hosts the deployment_failed dispatch so all three failure seams
  are covered.

said so; v2 did not open console-harness.yml and carried the digest's reading at
its own authority level; v5 did not re-derive the clause-(b) axis; v11 did not
re-verify the deploy-stage CLASSIFICATION half; v7 could not compare row
membership against the survey's 86-row undecidable list because only the count
was reported, so its 121-vs-86 is a set-SIZE comparison over slightly different
denominators and must never be printed as a subtraction. v1 could not execute on
a real GitHub runner -- its two CI shapes are FAITHFUL RECONSTRUCTIONS of
actions/checkout@v4's fetch refspecs, corroborated by console-harness.yml's own
committed comment, not observations. Two verifiers' evidence lives in throwaway
worktrees and is uncommitted; their exact output is quoted in the slice briefs
so a builder re-authors rather than re-derives.

[1;38;5;78mThe four things the reviewer FIXED rather than reported[0m

1. THE FALSE SENTENCE IN THE D307 GUARD'S HEADER (commit 73438f573). The shipped
comment claimed that deleting the scoping arm takes mutate_controller_test.exs
“from 36/36 to 23 failures, including the D53 create-family pins”. The builder
had already measured 1 failure and stamped an honest --miss on the criterion —
but left the brief's number standing in the code. Re-run independently at review
(delete `not cch_epic_child?(parent) -> :ok`, change nothing else): 46 tests / 1
failure, and that failure is this slice's own scoping test. The comment now
states the measured result and says plainly that the scoping is a DESIGN
boundary, not a load-bearing leg under today's corpus. An epic whose thesis is
“stop asserting what you did not measure” cannot ship a header comment that does
exactly that.

2. `:nxdomain` IS AN ANSWER, NOT A FAULT (commits 90ab9999a + 438495a96) — a
REVIEW DECISION superseding the letter of D332. The s7 builder built D332 as
written, measured that one fifth of it was wrong, and filed task-
3fbfff8c97b50c8f at priority 0 rather than quietly re-deciding. He was right on
both counts. Re-measured at review on this host: `:inet.getaddrs` of a non-
existent name returns `{:error, :nxdomain}` on BOTH families — which is
precisely what a freshly attached, still-propagating domain looks like through
the real transport. Shipped literally, D332 would have turned the MOST COMMON
waiting state into “we could not check” and stripped its propagation advice: one
lie swapped for another, in the very rung the slice existed to make honest.
NXDOMAIN means the resolver ANSWERED. Fixed with `@answered_empty [:nxdomain]`,
and — the half that matters for the standing test — the DNS test fakes now
RETURN nxdomain for a missing record instead of `{:ok, []}`, a shape production
never produces. task-3fbfff8c97b50c8f is closed with the ruling, its measurement
and its residual gap on the record.

3. THE REAP ALERT CAP IS NOW DRIVEN (commit ab2a6dce5). `@reap_alert_cap 25`
decides that the 26th owner in a cluster incident hears nothing by email — a
person-facing SUPPRESSION that no fixture crossed. The builder flagged it
against himself; the review paid it: a test fails 27 deployments in one sweep
and asserts exactly 25 emails plus the suppression log line, with
`Registry.reap_alert_cap/0` exposed so the test reads the real constant instead
of a copy. Mutation-proved: replacing `Enum.split(alerts, @reap_alert_cap)` with
`{alerts, []}` reds exactly that test and nothing else.

4. THE MUTATION ANCHORS IN s1's CONTROL (commit 52a80c7be).
`String.prototype.replace` on an anchor that has drifted is a SILENT no-op, and
`mutatedRun`'s own `notEqual(out, src)` guard would have been satisfied by the
roster mutation alone — so a future refactor of seal-predicate.mjs could have
left the floor mutation un-applied and the control passing vacuously. Each
anchor is now asserted present before use.

[1;38;5;78mLedger audit[0m

Honest across the board, with no corrections owed. Every built slice is
`in_progress`, published, with evidence stamped as the builder worked and the
merge-gated criterion left open for the lead — s1 6/7, s3 6/7, s4 7/8 (criterion
4 an honest --miss carrying the refuting numbers), s5 7/8, s6 9/10, s7 10/11, s8
7/8. cch-w28-s2 correctly sits `open` as a deferred round-2 slice. Backlog rows
filed by builders are published and open. The ONLY ledger mutation this review
made was closing task-3fbfff8c97b50c8f with the nxdomain ruling (criteria 0 and
1 stamped with evidence, criterion 2 recorded as an honest --miss and closed
over with a written criteria_override rather than graded by its own closer).

[1;38;5;78mGrade: A−[0m

WHAT EARNS IT. The wave's headline question was a judgment call between two
defensible options and it was decided cleanly, then executed without cheating:
#9356 is green in a REAL depth-1 clone with the thing that was red still red-
able, the fabrication sentences relocated rather than deleted, and an unanchored
regex that discriminated nothing repaired on the way past. Three builders
reported measurements that CONTRADICTED their own briefs — the 23-failure
prediction, the `returning:` prescription, the nxdomain classification — and
each filed the contradiction instead of reinterpreting the criterion into
something passable. That is the behaviour this epic exists to produce, and it is
why the review caught real defects instead of typos. Four person-facing bodies
landed with genuine fail-before proofs, and the cross-slice integration is
proved by a 2693/0 full-suite run rather than asserted.

WHAT HOLDS IT BACK. The instrument is still structurally blind in the only place
it runs automatically — s2 was deferred by the sequenced-rounds law, which is
correct process, but it means the epic is still certified by a predicate that
can only give a partial verdict in CI, and s3 turned that from an unstated fact
into a DOCUMENTED one, which by the wave's own standing test is a sentence, not
a fix. The D307 door guard shipped armed but its hard tier can reach almost
nothing (no fixture outside its own block carries a `surface` key) and its
absence tier is a warn, so the filing law still holds because people are
careful, not because a door refuses. s7 shipped a person-facing regression in
the most common domain state that only review caught — the builder measured it
and escalated correctly, but a wave that ships a decision it knows may be wrong
is one review away from shipping it for real. And the domain rail now goes
TERMINAL on `unknown` with no re-check affordance, which trades a spinning lie
for a frozen truth.

[1;38;5;78mWhat the next wave should take[0m

[38;5;59m• [0mMERGE ORDER, and it matters: round 1 first (#9356, then #9404-#9409, which are
  file-disjoint and all merge cleanly onto main in one probe). ONLY THEN cch-
  w28-s2 — it rewrites the same file #9356 rewrites and must mutate the
  discrimination out of #9356's fail-before controls.
[38;5;59m• [0mcch-w28-s2's hard constraint, armed as a tripwire by s1: HISTORY-UNAVAILABLE
  must surface as a verdict-token LETTER at exit 1, never a process-level exit-2
  INFRA-FAULT. An INFRA-FAULT prints a=UNKNOWN and will red s1's control —
  deliberately.
[38;5;59m• [0mThe REQUIRED `Console gate` depends on guerrilla.barkpark.cloud being
  reachable (one live spawn, test 56). s3 documented it; nobody has fixed it. A
  required gate that a third-party host can red is the same defect class as a
  guard no job runs.
[38;5;59m• [0mThe D307 guard's promotion: the `surface` ENUM plus the backfill of open rows,
  which is what turns the warn tier hard. Until then “every epic row declares a
  surface” is false and the code says so.
[38;5;59m• [0mThe s7 residue: a re-check affordance for a TERMINAL `unknown` rail, and
  domainRungChip's missing `unknown` arm (task-1daff7bc1bf46ceb) — a screen
  reader is still told “waiting” one layer below a rung that says it could not
  be checked.
[38;5;59m• [0mcch-w22-s7's remainder, finished or closed against origin/main's bytes rather
  than rebuilt.

[38;5;69m▌[0m THE WAVE'S OWN SIXTH CLAUSE, PAID TWICE MORE. Wave 27 added it: an
[38;5;69m▌[0m instrument pointed at the wrong ROOT reports defect-shaped prose for a
[38;5;69m▌[0m condition that is only “I could not look”. Wave 28 found the same shape in
[38;5;69m▌[0m two more places — a test bound to an exit code it does not test, which
[38;5;69m▌[0m reported a DEFECT for an ENVIRONMENT, and a resolver classification that
[38;5;69m▌[0m would have reported “we could not check” for a domain that was simply still
[38;5;69m▌[0m propagating. The fix in both cases was the same move: separate the
[38;5;69m▌[0m measurement from the absence of one, and say which you have.
